GAJENDRA THAKUR / EIGHT SOURCE EDITIONS

Diwan-e-Videha Khand 1

Gajendra Thakur · Ghazals · 612 PDF pages

The book and its concerns

The first volume of Diwan-e-Videha identifies 591 ghazals, alongside one rubai and two kata forms in the source structure. Its alphabetical index offers a second route through a large collection, allowing a reader to approach a title without treating sequence as the only organising principle.

In a sampled passage, crossing a bridge becomes a moment in which speed, the river and inward pain meet: movement between banks also invites reflection on moving through life. The closing verse asks the reader to examine a claim’s basis, compare sources and resist mistaking repetition for truth. Doubt is paired with reasons rather than indiscriminate rejection. These two samples show how lyric attention and habits of public judgement can share the collection without making every ghazal an illustration of the same theme.

INDIVIDUAL READINGS

594 unit discussions

These discussions include endings where the source unit has one. Each entry separates narrative or lyric gist from editorial interpretation and cites its place in the supplied Word edition. English headings are descriptive translations.

Reference locations count non-empty Word paragraphs in document order, including table text; these are not printed page numbers. These are AI-assisted editorial discussions; no named human review is recorded.

रुबाइ

रुबाइ

This rubai is indexed under “रुबाइ”. The unit works through a repeated lyric turn rather than plot, using the title phrase as the reader’s quickest entrance into its emotional or reflective field.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include रुबाइ, कारी, अनहार; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 656–660. Edition details.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include राति, मात्रिक, छन्द; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 868–882. Edition details.

Ghazal — के कहत

गजल ४४ — के कहत

This ghazal is indexed under “के कहत”. The unit works through a repeated lyric turn rather than plot, using the title phrase as the reader’s quickest entrance into its emotional or reflective field.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include मात्रिक, छन्द, पारम्परिक; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 1318–1332. Edition details.

Link to this discussion · मैथिली · Contents ↑

Ghazal — कोना होएत

गजल ७४ — कोना होएत

This ghazal is indexed under “कोना होएत”. The unit works through a repeated lyric turn rather than plot, using the title phrase as the reader’s quickest entrance into its emotional or reflective field.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include कोना, होएत, मात्रिक; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 1768–1782. Edition details.

This ghazal is indexed under “मौन सुनू”. The unit works through a repeated lyric turn rather than plot, using the title phrase as the reader’s quickest entrance into its emotional or reflective field.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include सुनू, मात्रिक, छन्द; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 3118–3132. Edition details.

Ghazal — मेहक बाद

This ghazal is indexed under “मेहक बाद”. The unit works through a repeated lyric turn rather than plot, using the title phrase as the reader’s quickest entrance into its emotional or reflective field.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include मेहक, मात्रिक, छन्द; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 4003–4017. Edition details.

Link to this discussion · मैथिली · Contents ↑

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include पीपरक, मात्रिक, छन्द; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 5308–5322. Edition details.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include नावक, मात्रिक, छन्द; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 5743–5757. Edition details.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include साँझक, घंटी, मात्रिक; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 6613–6627. Edition details.

This ghazal is indexed under “सएह बनलिऐ”. The unit works through a repeated lyric turn rather than plot, using the title phrase as the reader’s quickest entrance into its emotional or reflective field.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include बनलिऐ, मात्रिक, छन्द; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 7540–7554. Edition details.

Link to this discussion · मैथिली · Contents ↑

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 7981–7995. Edition details.

This ghazal is indexed under “एक परिणाम”. The unit works through a repeated lyric turn rather than plot, using the title phrase as the reader’s quickest entrance into its emotional or reflective field.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include परिणाम, मात्रिक, छन्द; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 8431–8445. Edition details.

Ghazal — रुचिक बाट

गजल ५४५ — रुचिक बाट

This ghazal is indexed under “रुचिक बाट”. The unit works through a repeated lyric turn rather than plot, using the title phrase as the reader’s quickest entrance into its emotional or reflective field.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include रुचिक, मात्रिक, छन्द; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

This ghazal is indexed under “सीमा राखू”. The unit works through a repeated lyric turn rather than plot, using the title phrase as the reader’s quickest entrance into its emotional or reflective field.

The gist should be read as orientation to a single lyric unit: rhyme, refrain and cadence carry much of the force. Source cues include सीमा, राखू, मात्रिक; no claim is made here to exhaust its prosody or every possible reading.

Source: Gajendra Thakur, दीवान-ए-विदेह खण्ड १, supplied 2026 edition; Diwan-e-Videha_Complete_591_Ghazals_1_Kata_2_Rubai_6x9_Hardback.docx, non-empty paragraphs 9316–9330. Edition details.
